Both adapters switch to the game-flow agent's `sim/commands.h` (`issue_to_selected`, `issue_or_cancel`) and `Battle::step` / `end_frame` by `__has_include` once that work is merged; the set-up adapter loads the AI agent's computer player (`ta/ai_profile.h`: `to_ai_type`, `load_ai_profile`, `collect_ai_metal_sites`) the same way. A trial merge of both branches built and ran the same scripted game (the computer player built extractors and a solar collector). Fixed while integrating: `Battle`'s motion hooks gave goal objects handle 0, so an order's goal counted as absent and was never detached when the order was destroyed (use after free when a move order replaced a build order); goals now get nonzero stand-in handles.

**Minimap orders** (assumed from `Minimap_CursorToWorldPos` `0x00498D00`, not traced): a command given on the minimap goes to the map point the pixel stands for, with no target unit; a left click there in the default mode moves the view.

**Open / not built:** minimap fog (the view player's fog composite built in the sensor turn), the RELOAD bars, TOTALUNITS/TOTALTIME regions, side logos (LOGO/LOGO2), MISSIONTEXT, the placement test of the original (here `structure_fits`), the pointer's own mission rules (`0x0043E490`), lightning/plasma shot looks, the unit info screen (F1), movies (intro, credits), the score formula, the TABMENU's own screens (allies, share, control: the multiplayer part's), the game menu's Help and Briefing pages, Ctrl+D's cancel of a pending self-destruct, slider art (drawn as plain boxes).

**Front end** (implementation: `client/front_end.h` entry points and runner, `client/menu.h` layout screens, `client/music.h`, `client/src/settings.cpp`; importer `ta_compat/src/client_menus.cpp` (art, sounds, fonts, maps, the battle session) and `client_screens.cpp` (the screens); `ta_play` starts with the menus unless battle options or `--direct` are given). Smoke-tested headless: main menu → single player → skirmish → loading → battle → Tab menu → Exit → Exit to Menu → statistics → main menu; options → visuals → screen size 1024 × 768 → battle at 1024 × 768; CPU = GPU (Direct3D 12) on every screenshot; a window run with sound and music. Not compared against screenshots of the original (the attempts to drive the windowed original were interrupted).
- **State machine** (`0x00426E80`, GS+0x2BBE top state, +0x2BBF/+0x2BC0 sub-state): 0 start (intro movie `1.zrb`), 2 main menu, 7 single player, 9 skirmish, 8/0xB–0xE campaign, 0xF/0x10 multiplayer and battle start, 0x11 battle, 0x14 lobby; a sub-state change runs the screen's set-up once. Movies (`*.zrb`, Smacker) are not built.
- **GUI loading** (`0x004A81E0`, gadget reader `0x004AD350`, 0x15B-byte gadget records): COMMON id, assoc, name, xpos/ypos/width/height (relative to the header gadget), attribs, colorf/colorb, texturenumber, fontnumber (0 hattfont12, 1 hattfont11), active, commonattribs, gaffile; per kind: buttons status, text (stages separated by `|`), quickkey (ASCII), grayedout, stages; labels link; the header crdefault / escdefault / defaultfocus. Button art: the gadget's name in `anims\<GUI>.GAF`, else in commongui; else (attribs bit 0x80 clear, gaffile bit 0 clear, no 0x1800 bits) switches take `stagebuttn<stages>` and plain buttons the stock `BUTTONS0` sequence (groups of four frames by size: 16×16, 321×20, 120×20, 96×20, 112×20, 80×20, 96×31). Kinds: 0 header, 1 button, 2 list box, 3 text field, 4 scroll bar / slider, 5 label, 6 surface, 7 font, 12 picture.
- **Gadget drawing** (`0x004A5F40`): plain buttons frame 0, 1 pressed; switches their stage, `count − 2` pressed; grayed: attribs 0x100 → last frame, else frame `min(pressed + 2, count − 1)` dimmed (−20 shade) unless attribs 0x80. Without art a raised/sunken box. Caption: trimmed from the end until it fits width − 6; vertical centre `top + ((bottom − text height) − top) / 2 + pressed`; attribs 1 left (x + 3 + pressed), 4 right, 2 centred (`(w − text width)/2 + pressed + 1`, the quick key's letter underlined), 0x20 centred at the bottom. Text height of the picture fonts = the 'I' frame's height + 2; their glyphs keep their own colours (frame n = character n, offsets = baseline).
- **Interface colours of a screen** (`0x004AC7D0`): each GUIPAL entry's nearest colour (|Δr|+|Δg|+|Δb|, first of equals) in the palette of the screen's picture; gadget colorf indexes this table.
- **Backgrounds** (`0x004288D0`, a ten-entry most-recent cache of `bitmaps\<name>.PCX` with their palettes, the palette applied with the picture): MAINMENU FrontendX, SINGLE singlebg, SKIRMISH Skirmsetup4x, SELMAP DSELECTMAP2, STARTOPT options4x, SOUNDS optsound4x, MUSIC optmusic4x, VISUALS optvisual4x, SPEEDS optinterface4x, ENDMSN outcome0 (campaign outcome1), HELP dhelp, loading loadgame2bg, statistics report scorebg.
- **Main menu** (`0x004263B0`, handler `0x00425D80`): SINGLE → single player (sound BigButton), MULTI → multiplayer (needs the multiplayer data), INTRO and Credits → movies, EXIT → quit; the music mood becomes 4 (silent). **Sparks** (`0x00425B80`, every frame): 100 sparks; an idle one draws a random spot (rand() % 640, rand() % 220) and starts when the picture's index there has low nibble > 12 (a circuit trace): life = rand() + 1 (byte), turn count = (rand() & 31) + 1, horizontal (±3 by x parity) when the life is even, else vertical (±3 by y parity); each step it restores the picture under it, moves, stops when off screen, out of life or off the trace (low nibble < 13), else draws index 0xAA; when the turn count runs out it switches axis (new direction by the parity of the other coordinate) with a new count (rand() & 15) + 1. The client draws these from the menus' rand() stream, whose state becomes the simulation thread's rand() state at the battle start.
- **Single player** (`0x004777A0`, SINGLE.GUI): New Campaign, Load Game, Skirmish, Options, Previous Menu (AnyMsn when all missions are unlocked).
- **Skirmish** (`0x0047BBB0`, rows `0x00479C50`, handler `0x0047AE60`, start `0x0047A760`): N rows (registry NumSkirmishPlayers, default 4; typing III…X roman numerals in the screen sets 3–10), spaced 200/N, first at (180 − (N−1)·spacing)/2 + 79; per row: Player name button x 45 (`skirmname` art), Side x 163 (`SIDEx`, frame = side), Color x 214 20×20 (`textures\logos.gaf` 32xlogos frame = colour), Allies x 241 40×20 (`TEAMICONSx`: frame 10 no group, 2g+1 a group of one, 2g a group of several), Metal x 286 and Energy x 337 (`skirmmet`). Kinds: open → computer; computer → player when there is no player, else open; player → open. Side: (side + 1) % sides. Allies: (group + 1) % 6, 5 = none. Colour: ± 1 (right click back) skipping colours other open rows use. Metal/energy: left +500 (cap 10000; 700 becomes 500), right −500 (min 200). Options: Commander (game ends / continues), Location (fixed / random), Mapping, Line of Sight (Permanent = off, True = type 1, Circular = type 0), Difficulty (Easy/Medium/Hard). Start refuses with a message when there is no player or no computer, too many players for the map, or every row is in one alliance; rows become player slots (controller 1/2, side, colour, allied with rows of the same group). Defaults (registry): rows open (a fresh set-up gets one player and one computer), side = row & 1, colour = row, group 5, 1000/1000; SkirmishCommanderDeath/Mapping/LineOfSight/LOSType/Location 1, SkirmishDifficulty 1, SkirmishMap = the first map of the list.
- **Select map** (`0x0047AAF0`, SELMAP.GUI over DSELECTMAP2): MAPNAMES list (the client lists maps whose OTA has a network schema), SLIDER, MAPPIC (the TNT's minimap picture), DESCRIPTION, SIZE; LOAD or a double click choose.
- **Options** (`0x00460160`; STARTOPT.GUI in the menus, PREFS.GUI in a battle with the `…RT` pages at 128,128): pages SOUND (SOUNDS/SOUNDSRT: FXVOL 0–64, MODE Off/Mono/3D, SPEECH unitchat/5, TEST), MUSIC (NOTRAK, MUSICVOL 0–64, TRACKMODE = cdmode − 1, TRACKTYPE of the shown track, CDPREV/STOP/PLAY/NEXT, TRACKNUM), INTERFACE (SPEEDS: GAME speed, SCREEN scroll 0–65, TXTSCROL 0–20, MAXLINES 0–30, LEFTCLICK interface type, UNITCHAT unitchattext/5), VISUALS (GAMMA 0–20, SHADING, ANTI, BSHADOWS, VIDSLDR/VIDVAL screen size); OK keeps, Cancel restores what was there when the menu opened, Restore Defaults / Undo Changes. In a battle the VID… gadgets are hidden. Values are those of the registry (below).
- **Screen size** (`0x004B5370`, `0x0045E4C0`, slider `0x0045BBF0`): the windowed list is 640×480, 800×600, 1024×768, plus 1280×1024 when the desktop is at least 1280×1024 and 1600×1200 when at least 1600×1200 (the full-screen path enumerates the display modes); sorted by width then height, modes below 640×480 dropped; the slider picks DisplaymodeWidth/Height ("%d X %d"); Restore Defaults sets 640×480 outside a battle. The client lists the display's own modes when the platform gives them (as the full-screen path does; a player's registry with 1920 × 1080 shows that path in use), else the windowed list. The menus run at 640×480; the battle loading switches to the chosen mode and the interface lays out for it (battle view (w − 128) × (h − 64)).
- **Loading screen** (`0x00497F40` while the loading thread runs): loadgame2bg; "Map: <name>" centred at h − 1.5 × text height; rows Textures 135, Terrain 177, Units 218, Animation 262, 3D Data 304, Explosions 347 (labels at x 90), bars from x 205, 3.5 pixels per percent, 20 high, interface colour 12 while under 100 %, 10 when done, the LIGHTBAR frame over them. The client reports one fraction and fills the rows in turn.
- **In-game menus**: F2 opens ARMOPT.GUI (the side panel at 0,128: Load Game, Save Game, Options → PREFS, Briefing, Help, Exit → EXITMENU.GUI (Exit to Menu, Exit Game, Cancel; Restart in campaigns), Resume); Tab does the same outside network games and opens TABMENU.GUI (Options Menu, Allies, Share, Control; Tab again closes it) in network games (`0x00495E90`, `0x00495010`, `0x00460CC0`). Esc closes an open menu, else the first press cancels the order mode, the second deselects. The client pauses the battle while a menu is open (assumption).
- **End of a battle** (`0x0041F7F0` states: copy the last frame, a 10-step fade, CD check in campaigns, outcome): ENDMSN.GUI over outcome0 with the victory or defeat title (the IGTITLES frames) at (w/2, 28); one row per player from y 93, 20 apart: the colour box (x 16, 91×21) with the name, seven bars 67×18 at x 112, 186, 260, 334, 408, 482, 556 (kills, losses, energy produced, metal produced, excess energy, excess metal, score), each growing by a fifteenth of its value per tick, one column every 10 ticks (sounds EndGameStatBar, EndGameScore; a click shows all with ActivateAllStatBars); MainMenu moved to y 416 outside campaigns. The score's formula is not read (shown empty).
- **Settings** (the registry values of `0x0042F9A0`, kept in a `key = value` file): Interface Type 0, DisplaymodeWidth/Height 640/480, Difficulty 1, scrollspeed 32, textlines 10, textscroll 10, gamespeed 10, damagebars 0, Sound Mode 1 (mono), fxvol 27, musicvol 32, musicmode 1, cdmode 4, unitchat 10, unitchattext 5, speechfx 1, Shadows/FeatureShadows/Shading/Anti Alias 1, Gamma 12, clock 0, SwitchAlt 0, UnitLimit 250, NumSkirmishPlayers 4, the Skirmish… values and per row Player<n>Controller/Side/Color/AllyGroup/Metal/Energy, CDLISTS (track types).
- **Music** (`0x004CDB40` pick, `0x004CE690` mood, `0x00490FE0`; the Steam version's `win32.dll` turns "play cdaudio from N" into `music\N.mp3`): modes (cdmode) 0 stop, 1 play all (next track when one ends, wrapping), 2 random (rand() % count + 1), 3 repeat the chosen track, 4 by mood. Moods: 0 building, 1 battle, 2 victory, 3 defeat, 4 silent (menus). By mood (also for victory and defeat in any mode): r = rand() & 15; a playing track of the mood keeps playing; else walking forward from the current track (wrapping) every track of the mood counts r down and the first time r < 1 that track plays; nothing found in (r + 1) × count steps stops the music. A mood change (in mode 4, or to victory/defeat) fades the track out (every 2 ticks the volume falls by 1/18 of its level), then picks at once, or after 120 ticks when the new mood is building; from silence it picks at once. Track types per CD (CDLISTS); a fresh 16-track disc gets tracks 1–7 battle and the rest building, otherwise all building. Battle/peace judge (`0x00494E70`, once a second of wall clock in a battle): a ring of 30 one-second slots gets +1 for each damage where the attacker or the victim is the local player and +5 for each kill the local player scores (`0x00494FF0`, the simulation's stats_activity); more than 10 seconds after the last switch: building → battle when (ring total > 50 or the 5 slots before the current > 30) and the local player has more than 30 units; battle → building when the total < 10, those 5 slots are 0 and more than 60 seconds passed. The client decodes the MP3s with the platform's decoder (Windows Media Foundation) and uses its own rand() stream for the music (the original's runs on its timer thread).
- **Unit notices** (`0x0047F850`, queue `0x0047FAD0`, play `0x0047FD70`, per frame `0x0047F680`, forget `0x0047F8C0`): table (priority, gap seconds, console text) by speech id: 1 select (10,0), 2 underattack (9,20,"Under Attack"), 3 activate (4,2), 4 deactivate (4,2), 5 ok (5,1), 6 arrived (3,4,"Arrived"), 7 cant (8,1,"Cannot Comply"), 8 unitcomplete (3,3,"Nanolathe Complete"), 9 build (4,2), 10 repair (3,1), 11 working (2,1), 12 load (7,1), 13 unload (7,1), 14 cloak (7,1,"Cloaked"), 15 uncloak (7,1,"Visible"), 16 capture (4,1), 17–22 count5..count0 (10,0,"five".."zero"), 23 canceldestruct (10,0,"Self destruct terminated"). Only live units of the viewed player that are not dying. A notice is queued when the game step has reached its next-allowed step and it is not already queued; the queue holds 8 sorted by priority (a new one goes before the first of lower priority); a full queue first shows the text of its last entry and drops it. Each frame the head is taken: if the step is still within the last spoken notice's length only its text shows, else it speaks. Speaking draws rand() on the simulation thread (always, also for text only) to pick one of the type's sounds for the notice (`<key>`, `<key>1`…) as rand × count / 32768; it sounds when priority > 10 − unitchat, the speech option is on, the effect volume and sound mode are not 0, at −5.85 dB; then the notice's next-allowed step = now + gap × 30. Text shows when priority > 10 − unitchattext: "<unit name>: <text>" on the console. A killed unit's entries leave the queue.
- **World sounds** (`0x0047F300`): need effect volume ≠ 0 and sound mode ≠ 0; heard when the viewed player sees the block of (x, z − y/2) (sight counts with the line of sight option, else the explored map); mono mode plays them centred at full volume; 3D mode places them relative to the view's centre (min distance half the view's size in cells × 16, max the map's width + height × 16). The client's 3D mode approximates DirectSound's placement with a pan by direction and an inverse-distance fall-off beyond the min distance.
- **More keys** (`0x00495E90`): ',' and '.' step back/forward through the focus unit's menus (orders, build pages, sound nextbuildmenu); 't' / 'T' track the next / previous selected unit in unit order (the view follows it; Ctrl+C selects the commander's category and tracks it); 'n' centres on the next own unit not yet visited (the on-screen marks; all marked → cleared); Ctrl+S selects the selectable own units on screen (every unit's marks cleared first); Ctrl+Z selects every unit of the selected types; Ctrl+<letter> selects the type category CTRL_<letter> (others leave the selection unless Shift); Ctrl+F5..F8 store the view, F5..F8 recall it; F1 unit info (UNITINFOx.GUI, not built), F3 jump to the last message's place, F4 option bit 0x80, F11/F12 debug; Enter opens the chat line (TALK.GUI over the top bar; the client sends the line through BattleSession::send_chat); `~ ! # * damage bars.
- **Middle-button view drag** (`0x0041CD50`): the pointer is held in place; every 4 pixels of travel scroll one whole cell (16 pixels) in the direction moved (also the right button with the drag flag in interface type 0).
- **Pick test** (`Unit_PickUnderCursor` `0x0048CD80`, `0x0048C6A0`, inside test `0x004C1320`): in the view rectangle, every unit of the visible list: the model's bounds' four bottom corners (min.y) turned by the unit's angles and projected (x + 128, z − rot.z − (rot.y + y)/2 + 32 with the scroll) must have the pointer strictly inside every edge; among hits the smallest (size_y·0x8000 >> 16 + size_z) · size_x >> 16 of the type definition wins (first of equals). On the minimap the radar dot with squared distance under 4 (nearest).
- **Game clock** (option bit 0x40 of GS+0x37F2F, registry `clock`): "Game Time: %02d:%02d:%02d" at x 130, y = h − 34 − text height. The victory and defeat signs (IGTITLES igvictory / igdefeat) are drawn over the view centre while the end flags 0x20 / 0x40 are set.
### Computer player — smoke-tested: 24 scenarios × 60 steps (1,440 operations: 353 controller steps, 187 direct role ticks, 65 regroups, 120 refreshes, 71 rescores, 53 picks, 53 site searches, 44 nearest-enemy calls, 858 profile command lines incl. 54 texts through the original dispatcher; 1,227 orders (intents 2:694, 3:29, 9:327, 14:180), 23 build menu commands, 82 activations, 1,131 placement checks), 0 differences; plus 1 predicted original crash confirmed in an earlier smoke run (large runs pending)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/ai.cpp` (`sim/ai.h`), wired into `sim::Battle` (players' stage, unit table refresh, creation and death group changes, `start_players`); importer `ta_compat/src/ai_profile.cpp` (`ta/ai_profile.h`: `to_ai_type`, `ai_profile_path`, `run_ai_commands`, `load_ai_profile`). Checked by `tools/orig_harness/ai_harness`: inside the suspended exe the real unit and weapon definition tables, three players (two computer players), units of random real types, a 64×64 map with metal and metal-spot features, fog grids; groups, controllers and tables made by the original routines; the original's controller step, role ticks (through the role objects' vtables), group assignment, table refresh and rescoring, pick, score, site search, nearest enemy, group changes, profile commands (direct and as text through the original's command dispatcher with the real category masks registered) run for real. Stubs answer from shared queues and log their arguments: mission resolution `0x43F0E0`, the order routine `0x43ADC0`, the build menu command `0x419B00`, activation `0x48B090`, the placement checks `0x47DB70`/`0x47D2E0` (plus the metal sum they leave at `0x51E688`), the range check `0x49AA80`, the explored test `0x408090`, the line-of-sight test `0x465AC0`; the weapon-target pass at the end of the controller step is silenced. Compared after every operation: stub logs, battle RNG, every table vector, builders, air bases, base centre, metal spots, search radius, site grids, controllers, every role's fields, all group lists and the units' flags and group fields.

**Where it runs.** Players' stage, per player with a controller: for an active controller-2 player the controller step (`0x408C40`) before its weapon-target pass. Table refresh (`0x40AA40`, every 30 steps per table) feeds the AI's counts, builder count, builder air bases and base centre; one time in 30 after a refresh the type scores are rebuilt (`0x409730`). Battle set-up (`0x464700`, per player with a nonzero controller byte, in slot order): the player's ten group lists emptied (`0x480190`); unless an active watched player, a controller (`0x408CB0`) and a table under the player's state index (`0x40B320`: site grids = **8 battle RNG draws**, then the scores). After all players (`0x464990`): the profile (`0x4648E0`). After the first players' stage of the battle (`0x497C4A`): metal spots per table (`0x4649D0`). Engine: `Battle::start_players()` makes controllers/tables for players with a controller byte; `Battle::ai()` gives the context for `ta::load_ai_profile`; `Battle::collect_ai_metal_sites()`.

**Groups.** Each player record has ten unit lists (the human control groups 1–9 share them); `Unit::group` (+0xAC) is the unit's group, −1 none. Changing it (`0x480250`) removes the unit from its old group of its *current* owner (search, then the last entry moves into its place) and appends it to the new group. New units join group 0 (creation); the death handler sets −1.

**Controller** (per computer player): regroup countdown (30), hold tick (the AI capturer hold, `PlayerController::ai_hold_tick`), roles for groups 1–9, each with a timer that runs it when not after the game tick:
- **Group assignment** (countdown, every 30 steps): every unit of the player's range with flag 0x20 (live units) gets standing orders (capture-able: move bits 01, else 10; fire at will); a group-0 unit goes to 1 (structure, unarmed), 5 (structure, armed: flag bit 31), 4 (builder type flag 0x40), 8 (flier 0x800), 7 (min water depth > 0), 3 (armed), else stays.
- **1 factories** (30 steps): each live finished structure: metal makers switch (off unless 2·metal < energy, NaN goes on; then on when net energy > 0 and RandMod(5) ≠ 0 — the same rule as the unused `0x408670`); idle ones with a build list (count ≠ 0, no orders) get the weighted pick through the build menu command (+1; names containing MAKENUKE/MAKEANTI would take the stockpile route).
- **2/6 strike groups** (300 steps; partner reserve 3/7, limit 20000/50000, sizes 3–6): rebalance (an empty group takes the reserve's first unit; while more than one unit, the farthest unit by squared whole-unit distance from the C-divided centre goes to the reserve when that distance ≥ limit × size — then the centre sums lose the coordinates of whatever the vacated list entry now holds, i.e. the unit swapped in (a quirk); reserve units within limit × size join afterwards). Empty → nothing. Size ≤ min, or not attacking and size < max: move to the base centre (armed structures, else unarmed structures, else constructors; group means) with **param0 0xA0**, attacking off; without a base: attacking on, attack order (intent 3) on the nearest enemy of the group centre.
- **3/7 reserves** (150 steps): move to the strike group's centre when both are non-empty.
- **4 constructors** (90 steps). Pass 1, units with a build list: capture-able ones only when the table has fewer than 5 builders and the hold tick is not after the step; head order flag 8 skips; pick a type; site search from the unit's position; for capture-able builders the site must be within ((world W − 32) + (world H − 128))/3 whole units of the base centre (flat, y ignored); build order (intent 14, replace, param0 type, param1 1) at the site. The site's **height is never written** (stack contents, often a heap address: input `AiEnvironment::stale_site_height`), and a capture-able builder's check copies it into the base centre's height used by pass 2. Pass 2, idle units (no order or head flag 0x4000; capture-able only with ≥ 5 builders): capture-able: base centre height := own height; offset to the base centre (y 0), replaced by a random 0x2800000 one beyond 0x2800000; move to centre + offset (= the mirror point), queued patrol to the centre. Others: distance d to the centre (x, y, z); d ≥ 0x1400000 → patrol to the centre; d < 0x100000 → patrol to a random point 0x1400000 away; else to 0x1400000 along the offset (scale = (0x140·2³²)/d, products >> 16).
- **5 armed structures**: nothing (its timer stays 0: runs every step).
- **8 aircraft** (30 + RandMod(900) steps, drawn first): under 5 units and a base centre: 2 + RandMod(2) orders to random points within ±(world/8)/2 of it (first a move, then queued patrols); without a base centre: queued patrol to the nearest enemy of the group centre (none → the original reads position 0x6A: crash, predicted and confirmed); 5 or more: patrol to a random point on a random map edge.
- **9 hunter** (30 + RandMod(150) steps; nobody is ever assigned group 9, but the draws happen): RandMod(10) = 0 → probe := best spot, random step 0x1400000; probe += step; seen by the player (fog: sight counts; else the explored map) → threat sum s within 160 of the probe; RandMod(best) < RandMod(s) → best := probe; attack-capable units (in weapon range when without motion) get an attack order when the intent resolves.

**Orders.** The group routine (`0x480460`): every unit of the player's range with a type and the group, in table order: the intent resolved for the unit, then the general order routine with the resolved kind (Ready included). No command layer and no network: the AI orders its own units directly.

**Table (AI part).** Per type: counts (finished own units, last refresh), threat, three build scores, base-centre weight (40 for build code 0 + 20 with a build list), weight % (100), limit (−1) and their "fixed" flags; builder count; builder air bases (builder + air base + activated); base centre: weighted float sums (sum −= pos·w·(−2⁻¹⁶), stored as floats each unit), divided by the weight sum when nonzero, ftol(·65536); metal spots; search radius; land/water site grids (margin 3: W = 8 + RandMod(10) + 3, H = 8 + RandMod(3) + 3, offsets RandMod(size) − size/2; water margin 6 with RandMod(20)).

**Scores** (`0x409730`, per type from 1): energy rate = energy use (if ≠ 0), else −wind fraction × wind output, else −tidal strength × tidal output. Threat = clamp(c + clamp(arms)) with c = ftol(ftol(base + metal cost × 0.01) + energy cost × 0.002) (base 1, 11 for extractors, +10 makers, +10 negative rate) and arms = 1 (11 attack-capable) + Σ weapons with an id: range/100 + damage/40 + 5. General = ftol((1 or 21) + 30 builder type with < 3 owned + 50 negative rate + 50 extractor + 25 maker + 40 flier + 15 sonar + 5 radar + min(energy make, 30)); ×4 with none owned, ×2 with one; ×3 water types; + threat/2 when the player's live units exceed half the unit limit; 0 for "can't build" (can-do bit 8), type flag bit 24, wind generators when max wind < denominator/2; capped at 100. Energy = clamp(float(energy cost × −0.0025) − rate × 5, 0, 100). Metal = clamp((extractor ? 100 : 0) + float(metal cost × −0.02 + (maker ? 25 : 0)), 0, 100).

**Build choice.** Score (`0x40BB00`, side = the controller's state index): 0 unless stored energy ≥ 50 and metal ≥ 25, 0 for downloadable types when the map info's provider is 1; energy need = ftol((min(ftol(capacity), 1000) − stored) × 0.125) ≥ 0 (+20 net < 1, +100 income < 50 else +10 < 200), metal need likewise with 500 and 0.25 (+20 net < 1, +100 income < 3 else +20 < 5); m = clamp(metal need, 0, 100), e = clamp(energy need − m, 0, 100), g = max(100 − m − e, 0); 0 at the profile limit; (g·general + m·metal + e·energy) × weight / 10000. Pick (`0x40BDB0`): over the build list, each positive score adds to a running total and replaces the choice when RandMod(total) < score; a choice of another side string is dropped.

**Site search** (`0x40BFE0`): radius += 160 while below the map's larger world side; centre = base centre, or (when the base is farther than the radius) the point at the radius towards it (scale = (r·2¹⁶ << 16)/d); extractors: RandMod(255) > surface metal → metal spots (`0x40A260`: spots within 4 × radius cells, score = −distance² in an STL heap, yard checks in heap order until 160 past the first fit, richest summed metal wins), else 30 random tries (`0x40A5D0`: random point within the radius, its corner cell snapped to the table's land or water grid plus random offsets, general placement check and summed metal ≤ surface metal × footprint × 2 — the sum is the last structure check's, stale for mobile types). Success: x/z footprint-centred, radius 0.

**Profile.** `ai\<aiprofile>.txt` from the OTA schema (else `ai\default.txt`), run through the console command dispatcher (lines split at `\n`, words at white space, `#` ends a line, 20 words, a 126-byte word buffer — longer lines never finish; first word looked up case-insensitively; unknown words go to the default handler: unit names matching it as a wildcard are created at the cursor, else `debugdat_<word>.txt` is run). "plan": the gate (a process-wide value, on at start) is on when a word names the difficulty (easy/medium/hard; "any" only counts as the first word). "weight X f": when gated, for every player record with a controller, types of X (a unit name → its bit and fixed; else the category mask) not fixed get weight = clamp(ftol(weight × float(atof f)), 0, 100). "limit X n": the same for active computer players with atoi. Then per active computer player, twice (weight-fixed, then limit-fixed types skipped), every downloadable type's `ai_weight` text runs with the gate forced on (the second pass runs the weight text again, not `ai_limit`).

**Engine notes.** The AI needs the battle's type indices to equal the definition indices (build lists and category bits are definition indices). `AiSystems` carries orders (`resolve_mission` + `enqueue_order`), the build menu (`adjust_build_count`), activation, placement (`can_place_footprint`, `structure_fits` with the new `ConstructionEnvironment::last_place_metal`), sight (`point_in_sight`) and weapon range. Faults instead of crashes: missing tables, groups outside 0–9, the aircraft order at no enemy, zero-size site grids, profile lines past the buffer. Dead code: `0x408920`, `0x408BF0`, `0x408620`, `0x4073B0`, `0x4074A0`, `0x409520`, `0x4095D0`, `0x40AD70`, `0x40BAB0`, `0x40C200`, `0x40C4F0`, `0x40C250` (weight report). Draw-time: `Hud_DrawUnitBuilderProbe` calls the score (no state change); the build cursor preview runs the structure check and so changes the metal sum that a mobile-type random site reads.

**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (name + plate comment, smoke-verified): `0x408C40` Ai_ControllerStep, `0x408CB0` Ai_ControllerCreate, `0x408830` Ai_AssignGroups, `0x4086D0` Ai_RoleFactoriesTick, `0x4077E0` Ai_RoleStrikeTick, `0x407560` Ai_StrikeRebalance, `0x4079F0` Ai_RoleReserveTick, `0x408100` Ai_RoleConstructorsTick, `0x407AE0` Ai_RoleAircraftTick, `0x407E90` Ai_RoleHunterTick, `0x407D40` Ai_HunterCreate, `0x407410` Ai_GroupCentre, `0x4071F0` Ai_NearestEnemy, `0x408670` Ai_SwitchByEconomy, `0x40BDB0` Ai_PickBuildType, `0x40BB00` Ai_BuildScore, `0x40BFE0` Ai_FindSite, `0x40A260` Ai_BestMetalSite, `0x40A5D0` Ai_RandomSite, `0x40A150` Ai_MakeSiteGrids, `0x40A7B0` Ai_CollectMetalSites, `0x40B370` Ai_CollectMetalSitesForTable, `0x409160` UnitTable_Init, `0x409470` Ai_ResetProfile, `0x40B320` UnitTable_Create, `0x409730` Ai_Rescore, `0x40B1C0` Ai_ThreatNear, `0x40BA80` Ai_BaseCentre, `0x40C230` Ai_BuilderCount, `0x406C90` AiProfile_Plan, `0x406DB0` AiProfile_Weight, `0x406E40` AiProfile_Limit, `0x406F00` AiProfile_Register, `0x409DC0` AiProfile_ApplyWeight, `0x409E90` AiProfile_ApplyLimit, `0x409F20` AiProfile_BelowLimit, `0x406EE0` AiProfile_BelowLimitSide, `0x480250` Unit_SetGroup, `0x480190` Player_InitGroups, `0x480460` Player_OrderGroup, `0x4B7A30` Cmd_RunText, `0x4B7440` Cmd_Tokenize, `0x4B7900` Cmd_Dispatch, `0x488C50` Category_LookupOrCreate, `0x40D620`/`0x40D670`/`0x40D700`/`0x40D740` ScoreHeap_Make/Adjust/Pop/PushUp. Read, not tested on their own: `0x4648E0` (profile load), `0x464990`, `0x4649D0`, `0x40B530` (builder air bases in a radius, for the aircraft behaviours; implemented as `ai_air_bases_in_radius`), `0x417890` (default command handler), `0x4BC370` (wildcard match), `0x435430` (profile path).

**Open:** the site height word in a real game (what the players' stage leaves at that stack slot: a run-varying heap address after the factories' role, so an input); other console commands in a profile (the importer only knows plan/weight/limit and the default handler); the gate's value carried over from a previous battle in the same process; the viewer's sample run showing commanders stalling on MobileBuild step 1 (wait 0xE0) was the route search, not the AI: its passability build faulted at step 0 (feature blocking flags never imported), so route goals that wanted a route were never served (fixed, see "Fixed in whole-battle runs"); with it fixed both computer players keep building (battle_view `--ai 2`, 18,000 steps: 64 units, no faults). The site height word does not enter the build order's reach test (x and z only); it only reaches pass 2's base-centre distance through a capture-able builder.

**Large run should cover:** `ai_harness <exe> <game> N 60 <first>` in shards (thousands of scenarios): army scenarios reaching strike attacks and reserve hand-overs with large groups; capture-able builders with ≥ 5 builders and hold ticks; the hunter with populated group 9 in both sight modes; extractors on maps with high surface metal (metal-spot search with ties); difficulties and providers; profile texts with every stock `ai\*.txt` file; long runs of the controller step alone (timers and RNG over thousands of steps); and the battle end to end against a captured skirmish with computer players.
### Battle start — the computer-player-free part checked against a capture of the original (battle generator identical at step 1); set-up routines read, not run in a harness
Implementation: `sim::start_battle` (`engine/sim/src/battle_start.cpp`, `sim/battle_start.h`), the importer `ta::load_battle` (`ta_compat/src/battle_loader.cpp`, `ta/battle_loader.h`). `battle_view --battle` builds its battle through them.
**`Game_InitializeBattle` `0x497180`** (runs on the battle loading thread):
1. `SeedBattleRng(QPC low + high)`, then `srand(time())` — the **loading thread's** `rand()`; the simulation thread's `rand()` is whatever the menus left (both inputs: `BattleStart::battle_rng_seed`, `crt_rand_state`). Game tick 0.
2. Options by provider (map info provider: 1 campaign, 2 skirmish, 3 network): skirmish takes commander death, line-of-sight type (map flags bit 2), mapping (bit 0) and line of sight (bit 1) from the skirmish settings and the unit limit from the ini; network games take them from the host side record's option word (bit 13 non-campaign, bits 11-12 commander death, high byte bits 0-2 map flags, max units), after waiting for the side records.
3. `Game_LoadMapTnt` `0x4917D0`: chat/order UI reset, textures, features, weapons, `Map_LoadTnt` (features placed: vents start their steam emitters here, on the loading thread's `rand()`), camera reset, game data, unit definitions, passability, **wind**: denominator 5000, next change 0, one wind step at tick 0 (which changes nothing: the change needs next < tick), tick 0, **meteor schedule** (`0x437CD0`), minimap, **every player record with a controller is set up** (`0x464990` → `0x464700` in slot order: economy, sight counts; unless active with controller 3, its computer-player controller and unit table — the latter draws the battle generator **8 times** (`Ai_InitRandomPlacementOffsets` `0x40A150`), for the human player too), the AI profile, the explosion tables (`0x4207xx`, ~400,000 `rand()` draws on the loading thread), end-game flags cleared. The end countdown is set to −1 by the front end's loading state (`0x498199`).
4. Skirmish: start positions. Without fixed locations the eligible players' slot numbers (active, controller 1/2/3, state ≠ 10, in slot order) are shuffled with the **loading thread's `rand()`**: fewer than three players → one draw decides (r×2/0x8000 ≠ 0) whether to shuffle at all; the shuffle swaps position p (p = 1..n−1) with position (r & 0x7FFF) mod p (never with itself: a Sattolo-style cycle; three or more players always shuffle). Then per eligible player in slot order `Map_SpawnCommanderAtStartPos` `0x496EE0`: side and colour bytes; storage bonuses = max(start energy/metal, 200) and player flag bit 0; the commander (side's commander name → type) at the start position with that id (y 0), fully built, layer 1. Start position ids come from the schema's specials: `StartPosN` → id N−1, a StartPos without digits → a running count − 1 (`0x437320`). Then the stored amounts (`0x465E30`: skirmish = the player's settings as floats, for all ten records).
   Network: per player with controller 1/2, **two battle generator draws** for a random spot (RandMod(width·16 − 160) + 80, the same for z), replaced by the assigned start position if found; no commander for a watcher; bonuses from the **local** side record's start values × 100 (≥ 200). Stored amounts: every record gets the **local** side's start × 100.
5. `Los_RebuildAll(1)`; campaigns: map-schema units.
6. HUD, `Lobby_BroadcastPlayerSlots`, **one players' stage at tick 0** (`0x464F80`: AI controller steps draw the battle generator; the economy's first turn), **the stored amounts again** (`0x465E30`), the computer players' metal-site survey (`0x4649D0`).
**Schema choice** (`Mission_SelectOtaSchema` `0x436860`, skirmish/network): types "Network 1".."Network 4" in that order, schemas "Schema N" from 0 until one is missing; a candidate needs StartPos specials; it replaces the current choice when its count equals the player count (highest filled slot + 1), the player count is 0, or it has more start positions than the best so far and the best is not the player count. Campaigns: Easy/Medium/Hard by difficulty. The schema also gives HumanMetal/Energy, ComputerMetal/Energy (campaign start amounts), SurfaceMetal, the AI profile and the meteor keys.
**Engine:** `start_battle` does steps 1-6 on a battle whose static data the importer loaded (types, map, features, economy, sight tables, meteor configuration); the computer-player parts go through `BattleOutside::ai_player_setup(player)` (in the set-up loop) and `ai_battle_ready()` (the survey). `BattleStart::loading_rand_seed` is the loading thread's state **at the shuffle**.
**Checked** (`game_flow <game> start golden_skirmish_moonquartet_300.jsonl`): with the capture's 38 computer-player draws replayed through those hooks, the engine's own draws (4 commander creations) and order give the battle generator state the original had at step 1 (`04deacf1`): **identical**.
**Open:** the network handshake (start position assignment `0x4568C0`, side records), the exact count of explosion-table draws (needed to derive the shuffle state from `time()`).

### Victory, defeat and the end of the game — read, implemented, not differential-tested
Implementation: `Battle::victory_turn`, `victory_stage_end`, `kill_team`, `deathmatch_respawn` (`engine/sim/src/victory.cpp`, state `sim/victory.h`, `Battle::victory`, `Battle::end_game`).
- Each machine judges **only its local player**, in that player's 30-step turn of the players' stage, before the settlement (`0x464F80`). Campaign: win conditions all true (`0x490230`, default "destroy all units"), else defeat conditions (any true, default "all units killed") — built and smoke-verified, see "Campaign". Skirmish/network: defeated (`0x490360`) when the local player has no live units (and, when the copy-protection check failed, at a forced step 9000 + r×9000/0x8000 — one `rand()` draw); only when not defeated: skirmish win when every other player that is not the local player's ally has no live units (inactive slots count as none); network win (`0x490080`, never in deathmatch) when every other playing non-watching player has made units and either has none left or is a mutual "allied victory" ally that is allied with every remaining player.
- A decision runs the **end countdown** (GS+0x39239, signed, −1 at the start): first 4, then −1 per turn; when it goes negative: win → end flags |= 4 | 0x10 | 0x20; defeat → deathmatch (commander mode 2) respawn, or a network watch mode (side option 0x40, mapping and line of sight off for this machine), or end flags |= 4, &= ~0x10, |= 0x40 (unless the lobby status is set). While the countdown runs (≥ 0) or the end flag (4) is set **no player's economy is settled** (sim/economy.h `settlement_allowed`). After the players' loop a network game (not deathmatch) without any human combat player left counts down too (`0x464F80` tail).
- **Deathmatch respawn:** up to 9999 tries: x = RandMod(W − 2·(W/10)) + W/10 (W = map width in world units), z alike (two battle generator draws); fits when the commander's footprint can be placed (`0x47DB70`, layer 1) at all nine probe cells (x, z ± the map's size in **cells used as world units**, a quirk), no feature at the spot, and on lava maps the ground is above the sea; then the commander, storage bonuses = side start × 100, and the start amounts credited as the commander's production (credit rule).
- **Commander death** (`Unit_BroadcastKilledPacket` `0x4864B0`, commander mode ≠ 0, playing owner): `Gui_PopUntilNamedLayout` (UI), then `Player_KillAllUnitsOnTeam` `0x486F10`: every live, not dying unit of the player: playing owner → 30000 damage of kind 3 from itself (`0x489BB0`), else explode as self-destruct (`0x49B000`), dying flag, killed packet kind 3.
**Engine API:** `Battle::victory` (`outcome` per slot, `over`, `decided_tick`, per-side network flags), `BattleSettings::provider` and `cd_mismatch`; `BattleOutside::player_victory_turn` still follows for the user interface.

### Commands (user interface and computer player routes) — the user interface's order routine differential-tested
Implementation: `sim/commands.h` (`engine/sim/src/commands.cpp`, `commands_core.cpp`).
- **There are no command packets.** The user interface orders the local player's **selected** units directly (unit flags bit 4); the computer player orders single units directly (`MissionOrder_EnqueueOnUnit` `0x43ADC0`, `Battle::order`); both only ever order their own machine's units (see "Network and sync").
- **`Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected` `0x48CF30`** (left/right click, order buttons, hotkeys, standing-order buttons): queued = the click's key flags bit 2 (shift). The unit under the cursor (GS+0x2CBA) is the target, and left out, when the intent takes one (every intent but unload 5, stop 10 and build 14; `0x43E470`) or, without an intent, when the mission's table flags have bit 9. Centre = the mean of the other selected units' whole-unit x and z (int division, ×65536 via `ftol`). Per selected unit in table order: the intent's mission (`Unit_ResolveMissionKind` `0x43F0E0`, with the **cursor's** world position GS+0x2CAA, not the given position); mission 0 → nothing; StandingFireOrder needs can-do bit 1, StandingMoveOrder bit 0; a point order (table flags bit 1) for a unit whose squared distance to the centre ((dx²)>>32 + (dz²)>>32, 64-bit) is at most 3000 × the count keeps its offset: position = given + unit − centre (y as given). Then `MissionOrder_IssueOrCancelMatch` `0x43AFC0`: queued and an order in the **primary** list of the same kind, the same target (when one is given) and within 0x100000 on x and z (when a position is given) → that order is unlinked from the list its secondary flag names (nothing if not found there), marked not-current unless it was the primary head, destroyed and freed — a cancel; otherwise the order is given (`0x43ADC0`).
- `Player_IssueOrderToMatchingUnits` `0x480460`: every unit (non-empty) of a player in group g: resolved mission, `0x43ADC0` directly (no cancel).
- Selection (`0x48C7F0` and family): own unit, selectable (0x20), complete, no transfer hold-off, not carried unless the carrier allows (0x40000000); a plain click clears bits 0x10|0x40|0x80 of **every** unit first; shift toggles.
- Engine `Command` (plain data, encodable) kinds: Select/Deselect/ClearSelection, IssueIntent/IssueMission (the routine above), OrderUnits (computer route), BuildCount (`0x43B0B0`), MobileBuild, AssignGroup/SelectGroup, TransferEnergy/Metal (`0x464B30`/`0x464C60`). `command::apply` between steps.
- **Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/cmd_harness` (the original routine with the resolution, table lookups and order call stubbed from shared tables): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 1,200 calls / 7,879 resolutions / 9,957 orders identical.** `issue_or_cancel` itself is read, not run in a harness.

### Network and sync — the original's model built (wire format differential-tested, smoke; games loop-tested, smoke); the engine's lockstep model kept as a test tool
- **The original is not lockstep.** Each machine simulates **its own** players' units (controller 1 local human, 2 computer); other machines' units (controller 3) only run their scripts and take states from the network. Packets (`Net_ProcessIncomingPackets` `0x453D40`, step stage 2; sizes from `0x451FD0`): 9 unit pose/create (0x17), 0x0A transport attach (7), 0x0B damage (9), 0x0C killed (0xB), 0x0D weapon fire (0x24), 0x0E projectile detonate (0xE), 0x0F feature damage (6), 0x10 unit script call (0x16), 0x11 activate flags (4), 0x12 builder link (5), 0x13 sound (0x12), 0x14 ownership transfer (0x18), 0x16 resource/explored-map share (0x11), 0x19 pause/speed (3), 0x28 player view state (0x3A), 0x2C unit state bits (`Net_DeserializePlayerUnitStates`, 3+), plus lobby/chat/status types 2-8, 0x15, 0x17, 0x18, 0x1A-0x27. Outgoing packets are batched per frame (`NetSendFrameState`, flushed in stage 10 when `0x506DBC` is set). The battle generator is seeded per machine from the clock, consistent with this model.
- The `Sync_*` family (`0x46CBE0`-`0x470790`) exchanges **unit definition (FBI) checksums** between players (data integrity), not simulation state.
- **Engine model** (`sim/lockstep.h`): every machine runs the whole simulation; only commands travel. A turn message = (player, tick, that player's commands for the step `tick`, its checksum of an earlier step); commands given at step t are due before step t + delay; a step runs when every player's message for it has arrived; commands apply in player order. `state_checksum` (FNV-1a over units, orders, motion, projectiles, players' resources, the `rand()` state and the tick; selection excluded) is exchanged and compared (desync = first differing tick). `CommandLog` records commands with their steps and checksums; `Replayer` replays one and checks the checksums. **All simulation settings must be the same on every machine, including `local_player` (the judged player) and `local_view`** (line of sight and EMIT_SFX depend on the viewed player, see "Draw-time side effects"); each machine's own player only sets the `player` of its commands.
- **Loop test** (`ta_compat/tools/game_flow <game> [steps] [seed]`: two battles from the same start with the same random commands; two "machines" over a reordering loopback with delay 3; the recording replayed on a third battle): **smoke: 5 seeds × 300 steps: identical, 0 desyncs, every checksum reproduced.** Patrol commands (`GAME_FLOW_PATROL=1`) no longer hang: smoke 8 seeds (300 to 3,000 steps) identical, no faults; the hang did not reproduce on base 9749015 either (seed 1, 300 steps; the use-after-free fix of retired order goals in 387e68a, made after the hang was seen, is the likely cure — not proven). game_flow now also fails on any engine fault (`Battle::first_fault()`).
- **Found with it (fixed):** use-after-free of orders and order goals: a replaced order's goal (and the route search) kept pointers to the order and goal objects, which were freed, so the "goal replaced" event landed in freed memory and runs diverged (ASan: `OrderGoal::report` after `free_order`). The engine now reuses freed orders **last freed first, with their stand-in address** (as a heap hands back the block it just took, so the event reaches the order now living there — to be confirmed against the original's allocator), and keeps retired order goals while a motion goal or the route search still points at them (`MotionContext::retired_goals`, pruned after each route search step).

#### The original's model, built (multiplayer, 2026-10-14) — wire format differential-tested (smoke: 6 scenarios / 133 compressions / 57 envelopes / 50 bit streams identical); games between engine instances loop-tested (smoke)
Implementation: `engine/net` (library `net`: `net/codec.h`, `net/protocol.h`, `net/frames.h`, `net/transport.h`, `net/net_battle.h`, `net/lobby.h`), the battle's network members (`engine/sim/src/net_units.cpp`, `sim/net_units.h`, declared in `sim/battle.h` "Network play"), bit streams `sim/bitstream.h`, game-data side `ta/net_setup.h`, client adapter `client/net_menu.h` (built when the front end's `client/front_end.h` is present), tool `ta_compat/tools/net_play`, harness `tools/orig_harness/net_harness`.

**Who simulates what.** Each machine runs the whole step for its own players (controller 1 human, 2 computer). The units of players on other machines (controller 3) only run their scripts in the unit pass (no weapons, orders, motion, healing or water damage) and follow packets. Everything random is decided once, on the owner's machine; the others copy the result. Each machine seeds its own generators from its own clocks, so the battle generators of two machines never agree, and nothing requires them to.

**Packet types** (type byte, then a fixed size per type from the table filled by `0x451FD0`; the unit state packet 0x2C carries its own size in bytes 1-2). Acceptance by game phase (table at `0x512BC0`): bit 0 menus/room, bit 1 loading (phase 5), bit 2 battle (phase 6). Sizes/phases: 2 ping 13/all, 3 3/all, 5 chat 65/all, 6 probe 1/all, 7 probe answer 1/all, 8 game start 1/all, 9 unit 23/battle, 0x0A transport 7, 0x0B damage 9, 0x0C killed 11, 0x0D fire 36, 0x0E detonate 14, 0x0F feature 6, 0x10 script call 22, 0x11 activation 4, 0x12 finished 5, 0x14 ownership 24, 0x16 share 17 (all battle), 0x13 sound 18/all, 0x15 loaded 1/loading+battle, 0x17 start position 2/all, 0x18 ready byte 2/all, 0x19 pause/speed 3/all, 0x1A unit sync 14/room, 0x1B status 6/all, 0x1C disconnect 5/all, 0x1D 9/never, 0x1E 2/loading+battle, 0x1F 5/loading+battle, 0x20 side record 186/all, 0x21 10/room, 0x22 6/room, 0x23 alliance 14/all, 0x24 6/all, 0x25 5/room, 0x26 slot table 41/all, 0x27 17/all, 0x28 view state 58/all, 0x29 3/all, 0x2A 2/all, 0x2C unit states ≥3/battle. Types 0, 1, 4, 0x2B have no size.

**Sending.** A packet "to everybody" (`Net_BroadcastGuaranteed` `0x451DF0`) leaves only from a present (+0x22 = 0) playing player (controller 1/2); a directed packet (`Net_SendPacketToPlayer` `0x451BC0`) also needs a present remote addressee (controller 3). With frame timing on (the default, global `0x506DBC`; switch `-p<n>` sets n packets per second, 2..30, `-p-` turns it off) packets wait in a channel: one for everybody and one per addressee (`0x513000`: 11 channels). A channel sends when its interval has passed (200 ms by default = 6 clock ticks of 1/30 s, `(ms·30 + 999)/1000`) or when forced, and at once when more than 0x429 bytes or 0x400 packets wait. Sending makes one frame per sending player: a 4-byte head (the everybody channel's running number: starts at −2 and counts down, back to −2 if it would rise above; −1 for addressed frames) and the packets in order. Without frame timing every packet goes out at once, unwrapped. Flushes: stage 10 of a step (not forced), and forced in many room and start routines.

**Message envelope** (`Condenser_WrapAndSend` `0x44FC10`, `Condenser_ReceiveUnwrap` `0x44F9C0`): kind byte 3 (plain) or 4 (compressed), u16 checksum, body. A frame of 13 bytes or more is compressed when compression is on (GS+0x4ED = 0) and the compressed body + 3 is shorter than the frame. Then every byte from offset 3 up to the last three bytes of the message is XORed with the low byte of its offset; the checksum is the 16-bit sum of those (scrambled) bytes. **The last three bytes are neither scrambled nor checksummed.** The receiver refuses messages under 4 bytes and bad sums, and passes other first bytes on unchanged. A debug loss rate (GS+0x37F35, normally 0) drops messages by `rand()` (no draw at 0).

**Dictionary coder** (`Squash_CompressLz77` `0x4D0F60`, `Squash_DecompressLz77` `0x4D1480`): window of 4096 bytes, zeroed, writing from position 1; up to 17 bytes look-ahead; matches of 2..17 bytes. Output: a flag byte for each eight items, lowest bit first: 0 = a literal byte, 1 = a little-endian word (window position << 4 | length − 2). A word with position 0 ends the data. The encoder keeps a binary search tree over window positions (node 0x1000 is the root, position 1 starts as its only child; positions are deleted 17 ahead of the writing point before being overwritten; position 0 is never inserted). Insertion walks from the root's larger child, comparing up to 17 bytes **as signed bytes** (the smaller side when the new string's first differing byte is less); a match at least as long as the best so far wins (ties go to the later node); a 17-byte match replaces the old node by the new one. Deletion of a node with two children moves the rightmost node of its smaller subtree into its place; links to node 0 can pick up a parent (emulated). After the end marker the encoder writes the item slot of the next flag bit from its stack buffer: an earlier literal of the same message or **uninitialised stack** (engine: an input; never read by the receiver; it can decide whether the compressed form is shorter). A "preset dictionary" path exists but its flag (`0x526FF8`) is never set.

**Receiving** (`Hapinet_ReceiveFramedPacket` `0x462F30`, split `0x463790`): per sending player a queue of packets with the step from which each may be delivered. A frame is split by the size table (stops at types < 2 or ≥ 0x2D or when the sizes run past the end; **a type with size 0 loops forever**). Its packets are stamped from the current step: one step per packet for up to `interval` packets, else spread `count·16/interval` sixteenths per packet, where interval = current step − the record's base step (never set after creation, so 0), clamped to 1..30 — after step 30 always 30. In a frame of more than 128 packets the oldest unit state packets beyond 128 are dropped. A queue holds 512. Delivery (`Net_PollReceivePackets` `0x4534E0`, one packet per call): the first sender queue (record order) whose head is due (not 1..30 steps in the future; at step 0 everything) gives it; only when none is due the next message is read; if that sender's head is still waiting, nothing more this time. Ordering: the head's running number should be the last − 1 (a mismatch calls an empty routine). The resend / out-of-order buffering of one frame is not built (TCP delivers in order).

**Dispatch** (`Net_ProcessIncomingPackets` `0x453D40`, stage 2 and while paused): per delivered packet: phase mask; the sender must be a known, present remote player (controller 3, state ≠ 10) and the addressee a playing record (else the sender is told "unknown" with status 6); the sender's last-heard clock and packet count update; then by type: 2 ping (empty answer field: echoed with the time, else the latency is stored), 5 chat (shown when the addressee is human), 6 probe → 7, 8 start (ends the loop), 9 unit, 0x0A transport, 0x0B damage, 0x0C killed, 0x0D fire, 0x0E detonate, 0x0F feature (0xFD destroy, 0xFE ignite, 0xFF reclaimed, else weapon damage), 0x10 script call, 0x11 activation, 0x12 finished, 0x13 sound, 0x14 ownership, 0x16 share (1 energy, 2 metal transfer; 3 explored map), 0x19 pause flag / game speed (1..20), 0x1B status (player leaving), 0x23 alliance, 0x28 view state, 0x2C unit states; the room types below.

**Unit packet 9** (`0x4861D0`, also synthesised by the state packets when a unit's type differs): for the sending player (its record must have a unit range): a live unit at that id is killed first (kill packet kind 0); then the unit is set up as a **new unfinished** unit (fraction 1, no hit points — remote copies look unbuilt until a whole state or 0x12 arrives) at the packet's position: field set-up (two battle generator draws: facing and hover phase), script and pose, weapon slots, metal extraction, a motion object for mobile types (heading = the type's build angle), ground height, footprint claim, sight stamp, the player's live and made counts, the victory notice. The packet's angles are not used.

**Unit state packet 0x2C** (`Net_BroadcastPlayerUnitStates` `0x48B710`, every step for each playing player after its units' pass; bit stream `BitStream_Write` `0x415C10`: 32-bit words filled from bit 0): type 8 bits, size 16 bits (filled at the end), step 32 bits; for each live unit of the player's range whose motion goal has news (route goal: changed bit 3 or the blocked bit differs from its sent copy; flight goal: network bit 0): unit offset in the range 16 bits, type index (bits = bit length of the type count, 9 for the stock 279), the goal's state; stop after the unit that takes the packet past 511 bytes. Then 0xFFFF, a 1 bit and the **whole state of one unit**: the unit at step mod units-per-player in the range (a rolling refresh, 250 steps per round with the default limit).
- Route goal state (`0x44F4A0`): blocked bit, waypoint count (0 without a route, else min(count, 3)) in 2 bits, waypoints x16 z16. The goal's sent copy of the blocked bit is updated, its changed bit cleared.
- Flight goal state (`0x4908C0`): order goal kind in 2 bits (0 none, 1 point, 2 manoeuvre; other kinds write nothing) followed by the goal (point `0x44DDC0`: flags 8 bits; follow: piece 16, unit id 16; radius / altitude / heading 16 each when flagged; position 3×32 with flag 0x20. Manoeuvre `0x44E930`: flag bit, position 3×32, velocity 3×32, target heading 16 with the flag), then the layer in 2 bits; the network bits become the layer << 1.
- Whole state (`0x48B200`): type index; for a live type: hit points 16, build fraction 8 (0 when finished, else 1 + trunc(fraction × 254) — receivers divide by 255), activation byte, layer 2 bits, carried bit: carrier id 15 bits and transport piece 8; else position 3×32, heading, bank, pitch 16 each and, with a motion object, its speed 32.
- Receiving (`0x48B920`): the sender's step goes into its record (+0x18, the lag the step budget throttles on, ≥ 900 steps behind). Per unit: a type that differs synthesises packet 9; the remote goal reads its state (remote route goal `0x44F5C0`: blocked bit into the motion flags, the waypoints; remote flight goal `0x490A10`: the old order goal is deleted, a new one is built from the stream (constructors `0x44E080` / `0x44E9C0`; fields not sent keep fresh heap contents: the point goal's position without flag 0x20 and its formation offset — engine inputs `Battle::net_inputs`), then the layer change routine). Then **every live unit of the sender with a motion object takes one motion step and a ground settle** — remote units move only when state packets arrive (normally one per step after the spreading above). Then the whole state, when its bit is set (`0x48B3F0`): type 0 → a live unit gets the dying flag; a different type → packet 9; hit points; fraction (flag 0x2000 when it changed, compared before rounding to float); activation (set the given bits, clear the others, with their scripts); layer; carried → the transport routine with the carrier and piece, else a carried unit is dropped; position, angles; a changed cell or layer releases and reclaims the footprint and updates sight; flag 0x10000; speed.

**Other battle packets (applied):** fire 0x0D (`Net_HandleWeaponFirePacket` `0x49D270`): a meteor-type weapon (flag 0x20) launches an ownerless shot with the packet's velocity; else a live owner's slot (packet byte 35) takes the aim heading/pitch, the target is looked up, an interceptor finds the shot it chases (another player's shot with the same aim point whose owner is the packet's target), and the packet's weapon picks the routine (ballistic / vertical launch / dropped / directed) that fires the **slot's** weapon. Script call 0x10: a live unit starts the function by index with the packet's count and four arguments (not run now). Activation 0x11 (sent by every change of a playing player's unit, `0x48B090`). Finished 0x12 (`Unit_FinalizeBuiltUnit`(builder, unit)). Ownership 0x14 (`0x488570` with the packet): the new local owner gets a finished copy with the packet's hit points, **build fraction sent as an integer** (`ftol`: a half-built unit arrives finished), attitude and stockpiles; the old copy is destroyed by its owner's damage packets. Share 0x16 is addressed (`0x456EE0` energy, `0x457050` metal, `0x4571C0` map). View state 0x28 (`0x4573D0`, every fourth 30-step turn of the viewed player in a network game, to the human remote players): the player's totals (its record's kill/loss words and resource fields) for allies' displays; `0x457540` applies them unless a local player shares that player's view. Alliance 0x23 (`0x452960`): a local change sets the own alliance byte (+0x108) and, for a computer player, its answer; a remote player hears 0x23 and the receiver sets its "allied by" byte (+0x113) (+0x108 too for computer players or when forced).

**Battle room** (`net/lobby.h`): the session gives each player an id; every two seconds (60 clock ticks, `Lobby_PeriodicPlayerSyncBroadcast` `0x456310`) each playing player pings (2: its clock, 0, its id), probes (6) and sends its side record (0x20, 185 bytes from its record +0x27) and 0x24; the host also sends 0x26 (the net id of each slot; 0 empty, −1 for state 4). Side record fields read by the original: map name at 0, map ready +0x8F, kind +0x94 (1 human, 2 computer), side +0x95, start position +0x96 (−1 unassigned), flags +0x97 (bit 0 host, 1 share metal, 2 share energy, 5 share map), option word +0x9B (bit 5 ready, 6 watch, 7 cheats, 8-10 = the map flags +0x9C: mapping, line of sight, its type; 11-12 commander mode, 13 non-campaign, 14 fixed locations, 15 closed), +0x9D bit 2 has the CD, unit limit +0xA5; start resources in hundreds (victory "side records"). The engine writes the player's name at +0x40, colour +0x99, team +0xA0 and start resources +0xA1/+0xA3: places not seen read by the original (they may differ). Start: the host's start (packet 8) after everyone human is ready, has the map and finished the definition exchange; start positions chosen in the room or assigned in slot order (the original assigns them while loading, `Net_HostAssignLoadingStartPositions` `0x4568C0`, not built). Then every machine loads with provider 3 (`sim::start_battle`): its players local, the others remote; battle generator and loading `rand()` seeded from each machine's own clock.

**Unit definition exchange** (`Sync_*` `0x46CBE0`-`0x470790`, packet 0x1A: sub byte, 4 zero bytes, key u32 at 6, value at 10): the host greets each human (sub 0); a client that heard the host sends its definition count (1), then four definitions per pass (2: key, checksum), then its count of host answers (4) every pass. The host enables a definition when its own has the same checksum (the original also needs every other human that sent it to agree; the engine compares with the host's only), answers everyone with sub 3 (key, enabled byte, confirmed byte, the unit limit short) and is ready when every human sent all its definitions. Key = the FBI file's simple checksum (def +0x13E); checksum (def +0x142, computed on demand by `0x42A610`) = XOR of the simple checksums of scripts\<unitname>.cob, every guis\<unitname>*.gui, download\<unitname>.tdf and the weapon section hash (+0x146). Applying (`0x46E160`): definitions the host did not confirm lose their "version compatible" bit (they cannot be built) and the confirmed ones get the room's unit limit (`ta::unit_sync_checks`; whether the original's gui enumeration names carry the extension is not verified).

**Frame loop of a network game** (`0x496790`): at most **one** step per frame (single-player runs up to 5); while the budget gives none and the game is paused: forced flush, network input and, every 60 clock ticks, a probe from the first human. The step budget slows when the slowest remote player's last state step is 900+ steps behind (`0x495230`).

**What diverges between machines, and how the original keeps it consistent.** Remote copies lag their owner by the frame interval plus the spreading (smoke: 3-8 world units for walking units, up to 30 in fights); between whole states they are extrapolated by the remote goal (waypoints or flight target) one motion step per state packet; the rolling whole state corrects position, hit points, fraction, activation and carrying every units-per-player steps. Random draws, effects, debris, wrecks and sight differ per machine; damage, kills, shots, creations, construction, ownership and transport are copied by packets from the machine that decided them, so the game outcome (who lives, who owns what) agrees. Remote copies start unbuilt with no hit points until their first whole state.

**Engine API.** `net::NetBattle` is the battle's outside (wrapping the client's own): `attach(battle)` before the battle start (the start already sends packet 9 for the local commanders) and again after it (registers the local players with the transport); `set_clock` (the 1/30 s clock, an input), `send_chat`, `send_pause`, `send_speed`, `send_alliance`, `remote_lag`. `net::BattleRoom` runs the room; `battle_start()` gives the `sim::BattleStart`. Transports: `net::LoopbackHub` (in-process), `net::tcp_host` / `net::tcp_join` (a star: the host relays; framing u32 length, i32 from, i32 to; a message from −2 registers a machine's players).

**Interoperability with the original (not built).** It would need: a DirectPlay service provider speaking the original's session protocol (DirectPlay 5 TCP/IP: enumeration on UDP 47624, session messages, system messages 0x0003 add player, 0x0005 delete, 0x0102 player data, 0x0103 name, 0x0104 session description — handled at the top of `0x453D40`), guaranteed delivery as DirectPlay does it, the exact side record (the bytes the engine places itself), the start position assignment and loading handshake (0x15, 0x17, `0x4568C0`), and the session description strings (`Lobby_BuildGameInfoStrings` `0x451090`). The packet formats, frames and envelope above are the original's.

**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/net_harness` (the original's coder, envelope (session send/receive replaced by stubs, import table filled with stubs) and bit streams in the suspended process): **smoke: 6 scenarios / 133 compressions (48 ending in a stale byte, compared but that byte) / 133 decompressions / 57 envelopes (24 compressed) / 57 unwraps / 50 bit streams (21,206 fields) identical.** `net_tests`: codec, frames, loopback, battle room (424 checks). Loop tests (`net_play <game> loop`, smoke): 2 machines × 300 steps; 3 machines × 900 and 1,800 steps; duel (each machine places 4 armed units near the next player: shots, damage, kills over the network) 2 × 900 and 3 × 900 steps: every owned unit present on the other machines with the same type, no stale copies of dead units at the end, positions within 3-30 world units; TCP between three processes on one host × 300 steps. **Not run in a harness:** the dispatch and every battle packet handler, the unit state packets, the room — read and implemented (named only here).
**Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`:** `0x4D0F60` `Squash_CompressLz77`, `0x4D0DE0` `Squash_TreeInsert`, `0x4D0C50` `Squash_TreeDelete`, `0x4D0C10` `Squash_TreeRightmostOfLeft`, `0x4D0B80` `Squash_TreeReplace`, `0x44FC10` `Condenser_WrapAndSend`, `0x44F9C0` `Condenser_ReceiveUnwrap`, `0x415B60` `BitStream_Init`, `0x415C10` `BitStream_Write`, `0x415DC0` `BitStream_Read` (created where missing: `0x460E20`, the goal network methods `0x44F480`, `0x44F4A0`, `0x4908B0`, `0x44EFC0`-`0x44EFE0`).
**Open:** the frame resend and out-of-order paths; the side record's other bytes; the loading handshake (0x15, 0x17, 0x1E/0x1F) and start-position assignment `0x4568C0`; watch mode notification (`0x450F90` from the players' stage); the team requests 0x21/0x22; the cheat warnings 0x25/0x27; DirectPlay system messages; the duplicate-id check `0x450980` and the time-out kick `0x453C20`; game-speed and pause keys of the client (only the packets are built); the TCP transport has no reliability layer beyond TCP's own.

### Effect particle lists — differential-tested: 24 scenarios / 3,226 operations / 5,428 sweep steps / 1,945 spawns (46 refused by a full pool) identical; 7 predicted original crashes (zero-length flames)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/effect_lists.cpp` (`sim/effect_lists.h`), `Battle::fx_lists`; harness `tools/orig_harness/fx_harness`.
- Ten ordered lists (GS+0x38D77, 10 × {?, begin, end, cap}); emitters come from one pool of **1000** objects (`0x51E610`, `Fx_InitEventNodePool`, free stack, shared with the nano streams; none left or the blocked flag `0x51E608` → nothing spawned). A spawn sets the emitter up (which emits once), then, if the list already holds **more than 400**, deletes its oldest, then appends.
- **Sweep** (`0x471EB0`, stage 9): per list in order, per emitter: finished → deleted (order kept), else its particles step, then it emits when due.
- Kinds (vtables): **flame dots** `0x4FD588` (teleport; 1 `rand()` per emission (start frame), every 10 steps until the deadline; flight = (int16)((ftol(len)<<16)/0x50000 >> 16), 0 crashes), **flames** `0x4FD5D8` (EMIT_SFX 0/1 on list 7 with (1, 6/7); burning debris at draw time: 4 draws (jitter x/y/z by r×3/0x8000 − 1 on the whole part, duration r×3/0x8000 + 1) before the pool is asked; no draws afterwards; a zero duration divides by zero), **wakes** `0x4FD5F8` (EMIT_SFX 2-5 on list 2 (size 16/8, 4-5 reversed), 0x103 on list 7 towards the sea surface; velocity = difference × (2³² / (2·ftol(len))) >> 16; each emission (2 in all) 3 draws jittering the start ±3 whole units; a particle goes when the step passes tick + size×6 or the ground under it is not below the sea level), **smoke** `0x4FD618` (impacts with end smoke, shot trails and EMIT_SFX 0x101/0x102 (smoke 2) on list 9: one puff; muzzle smoke (start smoke) list 9: at most 3 frames, 30 steps; burning features list 5; burning wrecks (15, 900) and explosion smoke above the sea (7, 15) list 9: a puff every period for the duration), **steam** `0x4FD638` (vents at feature placement, list 4: a puff every 5 steps forever, never finished), **nano** `0x4FD5B8` (list 6, see "Construction and reclaim").
- Smoke/steam puff: frames to live = r×(frames − 2)/0x8000 + 2 (frames = the smoke sequence's count − 1, clamped for muzzle smoke); each step the hold count drops and at 0 the frame advances and the hold is redrawn: r×(hold/2)/0x8000 + hold/2 (hold 7 unless given); gone at its last frame. A smoke emitter is finished when empty and past its deadline (unsigned).
- **EMIT_SFX** (`0x480EB0`): only for units the viewed player sees; the pose is synced; from the piece's first transformed point towards its second (types without 0x100) or the piece origin (0x100 types). Engine: `Battle::emit_sfx`.
- **Draw time:** each drawn frame starts emitters for smoking (smoke, list 9) and burning (jittered flames, list 9) debris pieces (`0x421550`); engine: `BattleSettings::draw_side_effects` → `Battle::end_frame()` (off by default).
- **Functions written to `/TotalA.exe`** (game flow; name + plate comment; smoke-verified; the emitter methods were created): `0x437DE0` `Meteor_TickShower`, `0x41C640` `Camera_AccumulateShake`, `0x41C6F0` `Camera_ApplyShakeDecay`, `0x471EB0` `Fx_SweepEffectLists`, spawners `0x471FD0` `Fx_SpawnFlameDots`, `0x472330` `Fx_SpawnFlames`, `0x472AB0` `Fx_SpawnFlamesJittered`, `0x472430` `Fx_SpawnWakeRising`, `0x472530` `Fx_SpawnWakeFalling`, `0x472630` `Fx_SpawnSmokeEmitter`, `0x472810` `Fx_SpawnSmoke1`, `0x4728F0` `Fx_SpawnSmoke2`, `0x4729D0` `Fx_SpawnMuzzleSmoke`, `0x472C50` `Fx_SpawnVentSteam`; emitter methods `FxFlameDots_*` (`0x4736E0` setup, `0x472D50` step, `0x472E70` done, `0x4737C0` emit), `FxFlames_*` (`0x4742C0`, `0x473010`, `0x473130`, `0x4743A0`), `FxWake_*` (`0x474760`, `0x473170`, `0x473290`, `0x474880`), `FxSmoke_*` (`0x474D50`, `0x475340`, `0x474F80`, `0x474DF0`), `FxSteam_*` (`0x475150`, `0x475600`, `0x4751C0`); `0x48CF30` `Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected`. Read and implemented but not run in a harness (named only here): `0x497180` battle init, `0x4917D0` map load, `0x464990`/`0x464700` player set-up, `0x496EE0` commander spawn, `0x465E30` start amounts, `0x436860` schema choice, `0x437320` start position lookup, `0x437D60`/`0x437CD0`/`0x438320` meteor configuration, `0x490360`/`0x490230`/`0x490080` victory and defeat, `0x486F10` team kill, `0x43AFC0` issue-or-cancel, `0x480460` group orders, `0x480EB0` EMIT_SFX, `0x453D40` network input.

### Fixed in whole-battle runs
Found by running `battle_view --battle` (with and without `--ai 2`), `game_flow` and the client headless for thousands of steps; each fix is checked against the original as noted. `Battle::first_fault()` gives the first fault any system recorded; battle_view prints it every frame, ta_play at the end, and game_flow fails on it.
- **Route search dead from step 0** (2026-10-14): `ta::load_battle` and the client set-up never filled `Battle::feature_blocks`, so the passability build saw every part cell of a multi-cell feature pointing past the (empty) table and faulted; a faulted search stops serving units. Symptom: computer players' commanders waiting forever on MobileBuild step 1 (wait 0xE0, route goal flags 0xB, search tick 0). Fix: `ta::bind_feature_blocks` (flag 0x40, the bit `0x47DE60` reads). The route search itself was already verified (route_harness, smoke again 0 differences).
- **Links to dead units never released** (2026-10-14): the kill packet's teardown `0x489740` was an unimplemented outside hook. Orders aimed at a unit that died kept their link and never heard event 8; an AirStrike went on to build a follow goal on the empty slot (motion fault, all motion stopped). Fix: `sim::release_referrers`; orders_harness runs the original routine for real (smoke 24 × 40, 89 kills, 0 differences). Named in `/TotalA.exe`: `0x489740` `Unit_ReleaseReferrers` (plate comment, saved).
- **Patrol hang** (game_flow): not reproducible any more (see "Network and sync").
- Smoke after the fixes: battle_view `--ai 2` 18,000 steps (both players build up to 64 units, no faults), the battle scene 3,000 steps, the client headless 6,000 frames, game_flow 8 seeds with patrols, sim_tests, orders/motion/route/combat harness smokes: 0 differences.
### Whole-game replay — a captured skirmish of the original replayed in the engine, the whole state compared after every step: 4 passive games identical to the end of their capture (400, 496, 2,759 and 3,000 steps; one with 5 interface orders identical for 2,220 steps)
**Tools.** `ta_compat/tools/capture` (`ta_capture --game [--auto-skirmish] [--rng-calls] --max-ticks N`, 32-bit, runs the windowed copy `OTA 31 Capture` under the debug API) writes the capture; `ta_compat/tools/game_replay.cpp` (`game_replay <game_dir> <capture.jsonl> [--steps N] [--continue] [--trace-unit ID] [--ignore-cells] [--no-draw-effects] [--verbose]`) rebuilds the game with `ta::load_battle` and compares. Run unattended:
```
ta_capture --exe "...\OTA 31 Capture\TotalA.exe" --cwd "...\OTA 31 Capture" --out g.jsonl --max-ticks 3000 --game --auto-skirmish --rng-calls
build\game_replay.exe "...\OTA 31 Capture" g.jsonl
```
`--auto-skirmish` makes the DirectX version check (`0x4B5070`, tested at `0x4266A5`) succeed so its notice never shows (start-up interface only), then posts the S quick key every 3 s to the game's visible windows until the battle seed is drawn (main menu S → single player S = skirmish → skirmish S = start, with the skirmish settings the registry kept). Only one TotalA can run: a second instance exits at once with code −1 (another agent's capture blocks yours).

**Capture format** (JSON lines; with `--game` the per-draw `rand` breakpoint is armed only from the first step):
- `battle_seed` (the value passed to the seeding routine), `srand`/`thread_td` per thread, `map` (`Map_LoadTnt` `0x483632`: EAX = the TNT path), `shuffle_rand` (loading thread's `rand()` state at each start-position shuffle draw, calls `0x4979E7`/`0x497A32`; the first one is `BattleStart::loading_rand_seed`; none → fixed locations).
- `start` at the first step's breakpoint: game state and unit table addresses (→ `settings.addresses`), local player/view (GS+0x2A42/0x2A43), map flags (GS+0x14281: bit 0 mapping, 1 line of sight, 2 LOS type), unit limit (GS+0x37EE6), AI difficulty (GS+0x37EEE), game mode (GS+0x37EF6), the simulation thread's `rand()` state (`crt_rand_state`), and per player record (GS+0x1B63 + 0x14B·p): active, controller (+0x73), net id, state (+0x146), unit range, stored energy/metal and storage (+0x8C/+0x98/+0xA4/+0xA8), the commander's unit name (its side), the alliance bytes (+0x108). Unused skirmish slots are **active with controller 0** (they take no part).
- `route_state`: the route search's scheduling state after the battle start (GS+0x2A3C player count, GS+0x1434F units per player, refresh counter `0x511A38`, search object GS+0x14207: player +0x78, cursors +0x79, credits +0xA1, served unit +0x58; scales `0x5119E8`, turns `0x511A10`).
- `cells`: every map cell with a unit, feature, flags or metal at the start (unit, unit2, feature, aux, flags, metal).
- `step` (`0x4954C6`, after the tick increment: generators at the start of the step), `digest` (`0x49566C`, the end of the step), `frame` (`0x495674`: after the frame's steps, also with none; generators, viewed player, map flags).
- `digest`: tick, `rand()` and battle generator states, wind heading/speed (GS+0x37ED8/0x37EDA), per player [slot, energy, metal, energy storage, metal storage] (float bits), per unit with a type index (unit +0xA6 ≠ 0; empty entries keep a pointer to definition 0) [id, type, owner slot +0xFF, x, y, z, pitch, heading, bank, hp +0x108, build fraction left bits +0x104, activate +0x10E, script flags +0x10F, flags +0x110, health % +0xF6, kills +0xB8, reload of the three slots (+0x18/+0x34/+0x50), primary order kinds (list +0x5C, kind +4, next +0x4A), secondary kinds (+0x60), motion [speed +0x20, turn +0x24, flags +0x2E & 7, goal vtable, route count +0x5C, search tick +0x60, route flags +0x64 & 15, points +0xC…]], per projectile slot (table GS+0x141F7, 300 × 0x6B) [slot, weapon index, pos, velocity, owner id, target id], FNV-1a hashes of the cells' feature bytes (+8..+12) and occupancy (+0..+3).
- `order`: every call of `Unit_EnqueueOrder` `0x43ADC0`, the interface's per-unit order `0x43AFC0`, `Unit_AdjustBuildCount` `0x43B0B0`, the build menu command `0x419B00` and `Unit_IssueOrdersToSelected` `0x48CF30`, with tick, return address, arguments (units as ids) and whether it ran inside a step (`in_step` 0 = the interface, between steps, applies before step tick + 1).
- `stale`: stack words the original reads uninitialised, per call: `ai_site_y` (`Ai_RoleConstructorsTick` `0x408100`: the site's height = [entry ESP − 0x2C]) and `reclaim_site_y` (`Map_PickReclaimSites` `0x47EA40`: [entry ESP − 8]).

**Replay.** Players from `start` (controller 1 local, 2 computer; side from the commander's name in `sidedata.tdf`; start amounts; alliances), options from the map flags, the seeds as above, `settings.addresses` from the capture, `local_view`, `draw_side_effects` on. The stale words are fed per step in call order through `BattleOutside::ai_site_height_word(player, fallback)` and `reclaim_site_y(fallback)` (new input hooks; an engine call the original did not make is reported). Interface orders (`issue_or_cancel`, `build_count` with `in_step` 0) go through `sim::command::issue_or_cancel` / `Battle::order_build_count` before the next step. At each `frame`: `end_frame()`, the viewed player, and a map-flag change rebuilds sight. **Between frames the original draws `rand()` outside the simulation** (lightning drawing, the CD audio sync, the interface): at a frame's first step the engine's state is set to the captured one and the draws are counted (1 in 3,000 steps of a passive game). Compared after every step: everything in the digest, except unit flag bits only the interface writes (0x10, 0x40, 0x80, 0x3C00000) and route flag bit 2 (below).

**Found and fixed with it** (each was the first divergence of a capture):
