1. **Map edge marker** (`0x4833B0`, called at the end of `Map_LoadTnt`): cells outside the visible area get feature 0xFFFD (blocks movement) where they hold no feature (0xFFFF) or a part (0xFFFE): the last two columns of every row; per column from the top while z·16 − height/2 < 0; per column from the last row upwards while z·16 − height(z)/2 > visible height (cells·16 − 128) the cell **above** (so the last row is never marked); on lava maps (map info +0xD44) every cell whose lowest corner ≤ sea level. Engine `sim::mark_map_edges`, called by the loader and the client set-up. Moon Quartet: 6,096 cells.
2. **Feature blocking was never wired**: `Battle::feature_blocks` stayed empty, so every feature blocked and every feature part raised a route-search fault — and with a fault set, `run_route_search` did one unit of work per step (its loop returns while a fault is pending): the original served the first build order's route at step 91, the engine never. The loaders now fill it from the feature types' flag bit 0x40; the targeting's feature flag byte (0x80 reclaimable) comes from the battle's feature types instead of `BattleOutside::feature_flags`.
3. Harness plumbing: unused skirmish slots, side lookup, start-position shuffle state, empty unit entries.

**Not compared:** route flag bit 2 — the route goal's constructor keeps bits 2–7 of its flag byte from fresh heap memory (here set for the CORE commanders, clear for ARM); nothing reads it. Motion goals other than route goals (flight etc.) only by speed/turn/flags.

**Results (smoke):** Moon Quartet, local ARM player passive, 3 hard computer players (ARM, CORE, CORE), 1000/1000, line of sight + true sight: captures of 400, 496, 2,759 and 3,000 steps (the 496- and 2,759-step ones ended by the process exiting with code 0 from outside — another tool's clean-up or the window closed — not a crash): **identical at every step**, generators included. A 3,000-step capture whose window received a player's right clicks (5 move orders and a solar collector build, 17 interface order calls) stayed identical for 2,220 steps; at 2,220 every computer-player unit gained flag 0x100 (radar mark set by the sensor pass, step 5 "the viewed player sees its block") in the original only — most likely a line-of-sight option toggled from the keyboard, which that capture did not record yet (map flags are now captured per frame and replayed); not reproduced since.

**Observed inputs:** the constructor role's site height word was, per computer player in slot order, 0 for the first and the **previous computer player's record address** (GS + 0x1B63 + 0x14B·(p − 1)) for the others, at every call (ticks 0, 90, 180, …) — left by the previous player's iteration of the players' stage; the replay feeds it from the capture. The battle generator and both `rand()` streams need no other input in these games.

**Open:** the air repair patrol's site address (`VTOL_RepairPatrol` `0x4152F0` stack record; not captured — no aircraft in these games), the hover bob clock (`clock_ticks`), the loading thread's `rand()` before the shuffle (vent steam at map load: none on Moon Quartet), network games, campaign starts, scripted input for the local player (the capture records interface orders; `--auto-skirmish` only starts the game), games long enough for combat (the original's computer players built 15 units in 3,000 steps), the `0x48CF30` selection path (only its per-unit calls are replayed).

### Campaign — goals smoke-verified (goal_harness: 24 scenarios / 4,800 operations identical), placed units' orders smoke-verified (order_harness: 24 scenarios / 960 strings / 3,531 orders / 68 attaches identical); the rest read and implemented
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/mission_goals.cpp` (`sim/mission_goals.h`), the provider-1 path of `sim::start_battle` (`sim/battle_start.h` `MissionStart`), `Battle::victory_turn` (provider 1); importer `ta_compat/src/campaign.cpp`, `mission_text.cpp` (`ta/campaign.h`); front end `ta_compat/src/client_campaign.cpp` (`ta/client_campaign.h`); tool `campaign_check`.
**Files.** `camps\<name>.tdf`: [HEADER] campaignside; [MISSIONn] (n = 0.. until missing) missionfile, missionname (plus language-prefixed variants). The campaign list is `camps\*.tdf` in enumeration order. A mission's map info is `Maps\<missionfile>` (an OTA); its map `Maps\<base>.TNT`. Briefing `camps\briefs\<brief>.txt`, narration and glamour sound `camps\briefs\<name>.wav`, hint `camps\hints\<name>.txt`, unit list `camps\useonly\<UseOnlyUnits>.tdf` (one section per allowed unit name). On the Steam install the original campaign's maps (`AC01`-`AC25`, `CC01`-`CC25` except `AC03`) are in `totala3.hpi`, which the game directory's ten-archive limit leaves unmounted; the original reaches them as the game CD's archives. `ta::mount_cd_archives` mounts the unmounted `*.hpi` of the game directory as that layer.
**Mission header** (`Mission_LoadOtaHeader` `0x435DA0`, provider 1, from [GlobalHeader]): maxunits (default 200; the unit limit), missionname, brief, narration, missionhint, glamour, glamoursound, UseOnlyUnits, mapping, lineofsight (the battle's mapping and line-of-sight options; the line-of-sight type is always the true one and commander death always off), memory, numplayers, Planet, nomovie, missiondescription (default "No description available"), wind, gravity, tide, lava, sea-level trigger, water damage, killmul, timemul, then the goal keys (below). **Schema** (`0x436860`): by difficulty the type preference is Easy, Medium, Hard (easy) / Medium, Easy, Hard (medium) / Hard, Medium, Easy (hard); the first "Schema N" (N from 0 until missing) whose Type matches the first preferred type wins, else the next type. From the schema: HumanMetal/HumanEnergy, ComputerMetal/ComputerEnergy (integers stored as floats), SurfaceMetal, aiprofile, the meteor keys, and (`0x436C30`) the placed units, specials and features.
**Placed units** (schema [units], children in order): Unitname, Ident, InitialMission (strings, 1023 characters at most), XPos/YPos/ZPos (whole units, shifted to 16.16), Angle (degrees; facing = (angle << 16, 32-bit) / 360 truncated), Player (1-based; 0 reads as 1), HealthPercentage (default 100), BuildPriority, CreationCountdown, MissionCriticalUnit, AiIgnore, AiPriorityTarget, InitialGroup, Immunity (bit 0 → unit flag 0x8000). Kills is not read.
**Battle start, provider 1** (`0x497180`): seeds as for every provider; options from the mission; the unit list (`0x431740`): when the useonly file loads, every type loses its "can be made" flag (definition word bit 23, `create_flags::kLoadable`) and the listed ones get it back — types not listed cannot be created at all (factories cannot build them, unlisted placed units are not placed). Map load, player set-up (two players: slot 0 the human on the campaign's side, slot 1 a computer player on the other side, colour 1). No start positions or commanders. After the line-of-sight rebuild the placed units (`0x488310`): each entry whose type exists — its player must be playing, else the original shows "Player number %d invalid" and exits — snaps a structure's position to its footprint, is made fully built on layer 1, then immunity, hit points = (max hit points × percentage, as unsigned) / 100, facing; after all entries, each placed unit with an InitialMission string gets its orders (below). No entries at all: the goals are switched off (the mission never ends by them). Then the camera goes to StartPos1 (presentation), the players' stage at step 0 and the start amounts (`0x465E30`): for each of the ten records energy/metal = the schema's (player 0 human, 1 computer, the others the zero fields after them), and the storage bonus = those truncated (at least 200, `0x496E90`).
**First orders** (`Mission_ParseInitialOrders` `0x487BF0`): tokens separated by commas (leading white space skipped); by the first letter (either case): **A** "x z" two numbers → attack intent at the point; else a unit name → attack-unit-type of that type (if known). **B** "name count x z" → structures queue `count` of the type (BUILDINGBUILD), mobile units build it at the point (MOBILEBUILD); **BW** "count" → BUILDWEAPON (no position). **D** → SELFDESTRUCTFG (parameter 1). **G** name → guard intent on that placed unit. **I** name → the unit is put into that transport (attach, piece 0xFF, layer 0). **M** / **U** "x z" → move / unload intent. **O** "m f" → move and fire standing orders (missing values keep the unit's). **P** "x z t" → patrol intent with parameter t × 30. **S** → MAKESELECTABLE. **WA** [name] → WAITFORATTACK on that unit (no name read: the unit itself). **W** "t n" → WAIT (t × 30, n). Positions are floats × 65536 truncated; y = 0. Every order is queued. Unit names match a placed unit's Ident, else its Unitname (case-insensitive, first in placement order, only units that were placed); a name that fails to parse leaves the token's own text as the name. Any order but BW leaves the unit unselectable (flag 0x20 cleared) and, unless A-at-a-point, D, P or S was among them, a closing MAKESELECTABLE is queued. **Stale values:** the two position floats are stack slots shared by all tokens and all units (a token whose numbers do not parse uses the previous ones); the importer keeps them across the units in placement order (`MissionParseState`; their value before the first parse is an input).
**Goals** (`VictoryConditions_LoadFromTdf` `0x48E010`, keys of [GlobalHeader]; at most 16 win and 16 loss goals, in this order): win — KillEnemyCommander, DestroyAllUnits, KillAllMobileUnits, BuildUnitType (name), CaptureUnitType (name), KillAllOfType (name), KillUnitType ("NAME,count"), MoveUnitToRadius ("NAME,x,y,radius"; ANYTYPE = any), UnitTypePassesX / UnitTypePassesZ ("NAME,v": column / row v >> 4), VictoryTimerRunsOut (seconds); loss — CommanderKilled, AllUnitsKilled, AllUnitsKilledOfType (name), UnitTypeKilled ("NAME,count"), DeathTimerRunsOut, AnyUnitPassesX / AnyUnitPassesZ (v >> 4). No win goal: DestroyAllUnits; no loss goal: AllUnitsKilled. The "NAME,..." keys are read with the runtime's scanner (letters only, then comma-separated integers in any base); a part that does not parse keeps what the parser's stack slots held — values left there by earlier keys, including heap addresses of earlier goal records (inputs, `MissionParseState`).
**Goal behaviour** (slot 0 = the player, slot 1 = the opponent; "walk" = a player's table entries with a type, in order, stopping when the filter says so): each goal has satisfied and cue flags; meeting it the first time asks for the "Victory_Condition" sound (presentation, `BattleOutside::mission_goal_met`). Polled: DestroyAllUnits (opponent's live count 0; flag not set), BuildUnitType (resolves the type once; a finished unit of it), MoveUnitToRadius (first check converts (x, y) with `Map_EditorPosToTerrain` `0x484B50`; then any usable player unit of the type in the circle, `0x47E890`), UnitTypeNearX/Z (any player unit of the type with |cell − line| < 3 — no other test), timers (tick ≥ deadline), AllUnitsKilled (set, then any usable player unit clears it), AnyUnitNearX/Z (an opponent unit within 2 cells of the line). Events (`0x4904C0` destroyed, `0x490520` transferred; every win goal then every loss goal; creation has no handler): KillEnemyCommander (opponent unit named as its owner side's commander), KillAllMobileUnits (opponent mobile unit; count mobile units, stopping at 2; fewer than 2), KillAllOfType (opponent unit of the type; same count), KillUnitType (count left > 0, opponent unit of the type: −1, below 1 met), CaptureUnitType (opponent unit of the type changes hands — called before the owner changes), CommanderKilled (player's commander), UnitTypeKilled (any owner; −1 without a lower guard), AllUnitsKilledOfType (any owner; counts the player's then the opponent's table, each walk stopping after reaching 2). "Usable" = selectable (0x20), finished, no hand-over hold-off, not carried or carried by a carrier with flag 0x40000000.
**Judging** (the local player's turn of the players' stage, `0x464F80`): when every win goal holds (in order, stopping at the first that does not; inactive goals: never) the end countdown runs as a win; otherwise, with active goals, the copy-protection draw (as in skirmish), then any loss goal (in order, stopping at the first that holds) runs it as a loss (end flags |4, &~0x10, |0x40).
**Editor position to terrain** (`0x484B50`): x and y clamped to the map (world units); from row (y & ~15) + 128 upwards in steps of 16 (at most nine), the terrain's screen row = z − height/2 (height = sample, at least the sea level); the first at or above y, then linear interpolation towards the point 16 lower; the y of the result is the terrain there. The two rows equal → division by zero (engine: `MissionGoals::faults`).
**Progression and front end:** a won mission sets the campaign's progress (missions completed) and opens the next mission's briefing; the menu offers the missions reached. The engine keeps progress in the front end's settings (key "Campaign <name>"); the original keeps a progress string in the registry (`Prefs_SaveCampProgressString` `0x432B00`, not reverse engineered). Briefing art, glamour and the planet animation are presentation (not built: the briefing screen shows the text and plays the narration).
**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/goal_harness` (the original's goal objects with their real vtables on a synthetic unit table; the sound stubbed; plus `0x484B50` on random heightmaps): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 4,800 operations: 2,385 checks (1,422 met), 930 destroyed and 490 transfer events, 995 conversions, all identical.** `tools/orig_harness/order_harness` (`0x487BF0` with the runtime's conversions and the real unit-name and type lookups; intent resolution, order call and transport attach logging stubs): **smoke: 24 scenarios / 960 random strings (well-formed and malformed tokens of every letter) / 3,531 orders / 68 attaches, all identical.** `campaign_check` (smoke): all 14 campaigns list; AC01-AC03, CC01-CC02 load, place their units (e.g. AC01 15/19, AC03 13/79), give their orders and run 300-600 steps. Not differential-tested: the header and goal-key parsing (`0x435DA0`, `0x48E010`, `0x436C30`), the unit list, the start amounts and the provider-1 order of the battle start (read).
**Open:** the briefing presentation (glamour, planet panorama), the progress string's format, AiIgnore / AiPriorityTarget / InitialGroup / BuildPriority / CreationCountdown / MissionCriticalUnit (read but not used by anything built), killmul / timemul (score), the "BetweenMissions" saves.

### Saved games — the bank container smoke-verified (bank_harness: 24 scenarios identical); exact save and load smoke-tested (save_check: 8 battles and 4 mission battles, every step after the load identical)
Implementation: `engine/sim/src/journal.cpp` (`sim/journal.h`), `ta_compat/src/saved_game.cpp` (`ta/saved_game.h`), screens in `ta_compat/src/client_campaign.cpp`; tool `save_check`.
**The original's container** (`HapiBank_WriteFile` `0x4B39C0`, `HapiBank_WriteAccount` `0x4B3C60`, reader `HapiBank_OpenBank` `0x4B3770` / `HapiBank_LoadAccount` `0x4B4270`): header 0x22 bytes: "HAPIBANK", u32 offset of the bank name in the string directory (0), u32 file offset of the directory, u32 offset of the first account (0x22), u32 version (1), u8 directory squashed, nine zero bytes. Accounts follow back to back until the directory: header 8 × u32 (total size including the header, section name offset, counts of integer, double, string and blob entries, 1 = squashed, 0); entries: integers {name, value}, doubles {name, 8 bytes}, strings {name, value offset}; blob descriptors {name offset or −1, index, absolute file offset of the bytes as if stored, size} (only blobs with bytes); then the blob bytes. **A named blob's descriptor keeps the previous numbered blob's index in that account, else a stale stack word** (the engine writes 0 and reports the offset). Names and string values go into one directory in writing order: the bank name, then per account its name, the integer names, the double names, string name/value pairs, blob names. A squashed account stores one SQSH chunk of the entries and bytes (size = chunk + 0x20); the directory is squashed when that is smaller. Reading: entries are applied in that order; a blob's bytes are taken at (offset − account start − 0x20) of the (unsquashed) entries and appended to the blob of that name / index. Keys and sections are found case-insensitively. Saved games use the bank name "Total Annihilation 3.0".
**The original's saved game** (`GameSave_SaveAll` `0x4326B0`): section Summary: integer keys "BUILD DATE: <date>" and "BUILD TIME: <time>" (0; the executable's stamps, assumed "Jul 30 1998" / "11:16:36"), maxunits, Difficulty, Side, Players, Gametype, for skirmish CommanderDeath, Location, Mapping, LineOfSight, LineOfSightType; BetweenMissions (campaign saves between missions); "Game ID", "Game Time"; strings Campaign, Mission, Map, Thumbs, Description. During a battle also: the radar image, camera, Players (per player stored amounts and statistics, a 0x1C "GameTime" blob), Units (per unit a 0xB8 record: type name, id, position, facing, hit points, build state, orders count, flags, weapon slots …; its script — thread slots, statics, pieces —, its orders, its motion's velocity/speed/turn/flags but not its route goal, its resource slot), Mapping (explored map), Features, player features, metal plot map, Meteor, the victory conditions (Satisfied/Celebrated and counts per goal). **Not saved:** both random generators, projectiles, effects and particles, debris, wind, the route search, motion goals, the computer players' state, and more. Loading (`GameSave_LoadAll` `0x432610` from `0x497180`, which re-seeds both generators) re-creates every unit through unit creation and overwrites parts of it; the game does **not** continue as it would have — so the original's format cannot restore an exact state.
**The engine's saves** use the same container with the original's Summary keys (so a listing reads them like the original's) and three sections of its own: "Engine Start" (what the game was started from: the battle request — map, schema, sides, players, options, seeds — or the campaign mission and difficulty), "Engine Journal" (blob "Journal": the battle's input journal) and "Engine Check" (tick, `state_checksum`, both generators at the save).
**Journal** (`sim::Journal`): every input a running battle takes after its start, in order — commands with the step they are due before and the number of frame tails run before them, the frame tails (`Battle::end_frame`, which changes state) with the tick at each, the clock values the hover bob reads. `JournalRecorder` wraps `command::apply`, `Battle::step` and `end_frame`; `JournalPlayer::run_to` replays onto a battle made from the same start, interleaving commands and frame tails as recorded, then `apply_rest` the commands given after the save's last step. Loading = start the game again + replay to the save's step + check checksum and both generators. This restores everything (scripts' threads and locals, orders, motion, route search, computer players, features, effects, both generators) by construction; the cost is the replay time (smoke: the four 1,500-step Moon Quartet runs, both games each, took about 4 s in all). **Requirement for the client:** every input must reach the battle as a `sim::command::Command` through a recorder, with each frame's `end_frame`; the current client gives orders and selections directly, so its sessions cannot be saved exactly yet (`ta::JournaledSession`; the save screen says so).
**Verified:** `tools/orig_harness/bank_harness` (random stores built in the original's store, `0x4B3750`/`0x4B4560`/`0x4B4630`/`0x4B46C0`/`0x4B4750`/`0x4B4BA0`/`0x4B4B50`/`0x4B4CF0`, written stored and squashed by `0x4B39C0`; the runtime's start-up run first, its fatal-error paths made to fault): **smoke: 24 scenarios, 270 keys, 98 blobs: header and accounts byte for byte identical with `ta::write_bank` (60,173 bytes, stale words excepted), both files read back by `ta::read_bank` identical to the content (18 squashed directories, every squashed account).** `save_check` (save at N, continue to M; load at N, continue to M; per step `state_checksum`, both generators and a deeper hash of scripts' thread memory, statics and piece animations, motion, economy, players): **smoke: Moon Quartet 4 × (900 → 1,500) and 4 × (600 → 1,200), AC03 2 × (300 → 600), CC02 2 × (300 → 600): every step identical.**
**Open:** restoring a game from the original's own sections (their layouts are read above, not the unit record in detail); writing the original's own sections from an engine battle; squashing on write; the client's input journal (see the requirement above); the original's save file names and directory.
